Essential for preprocessing raw e-commerce data.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### 1. Handling Missing Values\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Identify missing values\ndf.isnull().sum()\ndf.isnull().sum() / len(df) * 100  # percentage\n\n# Drop rows with any missing values\ndf_clean = df.dropna()\n\n# Drop rows missing in specific columns\ndf_clean = df.dropna(subset=['column1', 'column2'])\n\n# Forward/backward fill for time series\ndf['column'] = df['column'].fillna(method='ffill')\n\n# Fill with statistical values\ndf['column'] = df['column'].fillna(df['column'].median())\n```\n\n### 2. Removing Duplicates\n```python\n# Identify exact duplicates\nduplicates = df[df.duplicated()]\n\n# Remove exact duplicates\ndf_clean = df.drop_duplicates()\n\n# Remove duplicates based on specific columns\ndf_clean = df.drop_duplicates(subset=['id', 'date'])\n\n# Keep first/last occurrence\ndf_clean = df.drop_duplicates(subset=['id'], keep='first')\n```\n\n### 3. Data Type Standardization\n```python\n# Convert data types\ndf['date_column'] = pd.to_datetime(df['date_column'])\ndf['numeric'] = pd.to_numeric(df['numeric'], errors='coerce')\ndf['category'] = df['category'].astype('category')\n\n# Standardize string formatting\ndf['text'] = df['text'].str.strip().str.lower()\ndf['text'] = df['text'].str.replace(r'\\s+', ' ', regex=True)\n```\n\n### 4. Outlier Detection and Handling\n```python\n# IQR method\nQ1 = df['column'].quantile(0.25)\nQ3 = df['column'].quantile(0.75)\nIQR = Q3 - Q1\noutliers = df[(df['column'] < Q1 - 1.5*IQR) | (df['column'] > Q3 + 1.5*IQR)]\n\n# Z-score method\nfrom scipy import stats\nz_scores = np.abs(stats.zscore(df['column']))\ndf_clean = df[z_scores < 3]\n\n# Cap outliers instead of removing\ndf['column'] = df['column'].clip(lower=Q1 - 1.5*IQR, upper=Q3 + 1.5*IQR)\n```\n\n### 5. regex=True)\n\n# Standardize delimiters\ndf['value'] = df['value'].str.replace(',', '.')\n\n# Split and extract\ndf[['first', 'last']] = df['name'].str.split(' ', n=1, expand=True)\n```\n\n## 0]\n\n# Remove duplicates within reasonable tolerance\ntransactions = transactions.sort_values('purchase_date').drop_duplicates(\n    subset=['customer_id', 'product_id', 'purchase_date'],\n print(f\"Memory usage: {df.memory_usage().sum() / 1024**2:.2f} MB\")\n\nvalidate_data(transactions, \"Transactions\")\nvalidate_data(survey, \"Survey\")\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- **Document {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01WNm2kpqTrabfFWcYtrukYK","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01KUzTuAVhKn4UyXzWDcUo3j","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/environment/skills/time-series-anomaly-detection.md","content":"# Time Series Anomaly Detection and Counterfactual Analysis\n\n## Overview\nTechniques for identifying unusual patterns in time series data using statistical methods and counterfactual predictions. Essential for detecting anomalous sales trends.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy scipy statsmodels scikit-learn\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### 1. Baseline Estimation with LOESS\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom statsmodels.nonparametric.smoothers_lowess import lowess\n\n# Smooth historical data to estimate expected trend\nhistory_dates = pd.date_range('2019-01-01', '2020-02-29', freq='D')\nhistorical_sales = np.random.randn(len(history_dates)).cumsum()\n\n# LOESS smoothing for trend extraction\nresult = lowess(historical_sales, np.arange(len(history_dates)), frac=0.1)\ntrend = result[:, 1]\n\n# Residuals (noise around trend)\nresiduals = historical_sales - trend\n```\n\n### 2. Statistical Methods for Anomaly Detection\n\n#### Z-Score Method\n```python\ndef detect_anomalies_zscore(series, threshold=2.5):\n    \"\"\"Detect anomalies using Z-score\"\"\"\n    mean = series.mean()\n    std = series.std()\n    z_scores = np.abs((series - mean) / std)\n    return z_scores > threshold\n\n# Apply to daily sales\nanomalies = detect_anomalies_zscore(daily_sales)\n```\n\n#### IQR Method\n```python\ndef detect_anomalies_iqr(series, k=1.5):\n    \"\"\"Detect anomalies using Interquartile Range\"\"\"\n    Q1 = series.quantile(0.25)\n    Q3 = series.quantile(0.75)\n    IQR = Q3 - Q1\n    lower_bound = Q1 - k * IQR\n    upper_bound = Q3 + k * IQR\n    return (series < lower_bound) | (series > upper_bound)\n\nanomalies = detect_anomalies_iqr(daily_sales, k=1.5)\n```\n\n### 3. Counterfactual Prediction\n\n#### Time Series Decomposition\n```python\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.seasonal import seasonal_decompose\n\n# Decompose into trend, seasonal, and residual components\ndecomposition = seasonal_decompose(historical_sales, model='additive', period=365)\ntrend = decomposition.trend\nseasonal = decomposition.seasonal\nresidual = decomposition.resid\n\n# Predict March 2020 counterfactual (what would have happened)\n# assuming no treatment effect\npredicted_march = trend.iloc[-1] + seasonal['2020-03']\n```\n\n#### ARIMA Method\n```python\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.arima.model import ARIMA\n\n# Fit ARIMA on historical data\nmodel = ARIMA(historical_sales, order=(5, 1, 2))\nresults = model.fit()\n\n# Forecast March 2020\nforecast = results.get_forecast(steps=31)\npredicted_march = forecast.predicted_mean\nconfidence_intervals = forecast.conf_int()\n```\n\n### 4. Deviation Index Calculation\n\n```python\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(observed, predicted, tolerance=0.1):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly index from -100 to 100\n    100 = very unusual increase\n    0 = normal\n    -100 = unusual decrease\n    \"\"\"\n    pct_change = (observed - predicted) / (predicted + 1e-6)\n\n    if pct_change > 0:\n        # Surge: scale positive deviations to 0-100\n        anomaly_index = min(100, (pct_change / (pct_change + tolerance)) * 100)\n    else:\n        # Slump: scale negative deviations to -100-0\n        anomaly_index = max(-100, (pct_change / (abs(pct_change) + tolerance)) * 100)\n\n    return anomaly_index\n\n# Apply to all categories\ncategory_anomalies = {}\nfor category in categories:\n    observed = march_sales[category].sum()\n    predicted = counterfactual[category]\n    anomaly_index = calculate_anomaly_index(observed, predicted)\n    category_anomalies[category] = anomaly_index\n```\n\n### 5. Multi-Step Anomaly Pipeline\n\n```python\ndef detect_category_anomalies(daily_sales_df, target_period='2020-03'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete pipeline for category-level anomaly detection\n\n    daily_sales_df: DataFrame with columns [date, category, sales]\n    target_period: str in 'YYYY-MM' format\n    \"\"\"\n    anomalies = {}\n\n    for category in daily_sales_df['category'].unique():\n        # Filter historical data (before target period)\n        category_data = daily_sales_df[daily_sales_df['category'] == category].copy()\n        category_data['date'] = pd.to_datetime(category_data['date'])\n\n        baseline = category_data[category_data['date'] < f'{target_period}-01']\n        target = category_data[category_data['date'].dt.to_period('M') == target_period]\n\n        # Estimate counterfactual for target period\n        # Method 1: Average of baseline period\n        counterfactual_mean = baseline['sales'].mean()\n\n        # Method 2: Seasonal adjustment\n        baseline['month'] = baseline['date'].dt.month\n        seasonal_factor = baseline[baseline['month'] == 3]['sales'].mean() / baseline['sales'].mean()\n        counterfactual_seasonal = counterfactual_mean * seasonal_factor\n\n        # Calculate actual vs predicted\n        actual_sales = target['sales'].sum()\n        predicted_sales = counterfactual_seasonal * len(target)\n\n        # Compute anomaly index\n        anomaly_idx = calculate_anomaly_index(actual_sales, predicted_sales)\n        anomalies[category] = {\n            'anomaly_index': anomaly_idx,\n            'actual': actual_sales,\n            'predicted': predicted_sales,\n            'n_days': len(target)\n        }\n\n    return anomalies\n```\n\n### 6. Robustness Checks\n\n```python\ndef validate_anomalies(daily_sales_df, anomalies):\n    \"\"\"Validate anomaly detection results\"\"\"\n\n    results = []\n    for category, metrics in anomalies.items():\n        # Check if anomaly is statistically significant\n        category_data = daily_sales_df[daily_sales_df['category'] == category]\n        baseline_std = category_data['sales'].std()\n\n        deviation = abs(metrics['actual'] - metrics['predicted'])\n        z_score = deviation / (baseline_std + 1e-6)\n\n        significant = z_score > 2.0  # 95% confidence\n\n        results.append({\n            'category': category,\n            'anomaly_index': metrics['anomaly_index'],\n            'z_score': z_score,\n            'significant': significant\n        })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n## Practical Example: E-Commerce Category Analysis\n\n```python\n# Load transaction data\ntransactions = pd.read_csv('transactions.csv')\ntransactions['date'] = pd.to_datetime(transactions['date'])\ntransactions['category'] = transactions['category'].str.lower().str.strip()\n\n# Create daily sales by category\ndaily_sales = transactions.groupby(['date', 'category']).agg({\n    'amount': 'sum',\n    'order_id': 'count'\n}).reset_index()\ndaily_sales.columns = ['date', 'category', 'sales', 'orders']\n\n# Detect anomalies in March 2020\nanomalies = detect_category_anomalies(daily_sales, target_period='2020-03')\n\n# Convert to DataFrame and sort by anomaly index\nanomaly_df = pd.DataFrame([\n    {'category': cat, 'anomaly_index': metrics['anomaly_index']}\n    for cat, metrics in anomalies.items()\n]).sort_values('anomaly_index', ascending=False)\n\n# Top 10 surges\ntop_surges = anomaly_df[anomaly_df['anomaly_index'] > 0].head(10)\n\n# Top 10 slumps\ntop_slumps = anomaly_df[anomaly_df['anomaly_index'] < 0].tail(10).sort_values('anomaly_index')\n\nprint(top_surges)\nprint(top_slumps)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- **Use multiple methods**: Combine ARIMA, LOESS, and seasonal decomposition\n- **Account Essential for detecting anomalous sales trends.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy scipy statsmodels scikit-learn\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### 1. Baseline Estimation with LOESS\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom statsmodels.nonparametric.smoothers_lowess import lowess\n\n# Smooth historical data to estimate expected trend\nhistory_dates = pd.date_range('2019-01-01', '2020-02-29', freq='D')\nhistorical_sales = np.random.randn(len(history_dates)).cumsum()\n\n# LOESS smoothing for trend extraction\nresult = lowess(historical_sales, np.arange(len(history_dates)), frac=0.1)\ntrend = result[:, 1]\n\n# Residuals (noise around trend)\nresiduals = historical_sales - trend\n```\n\n### 2. Statistical Methods for Anomaly Detection\n\n#### Z-Score Method\n```python\ndef detect_anomalies_zscore(series, threshold=2.5):\n    \"\"\"Detect anomalies using Z-score\"\"\"\n    mean = series.mean()\n    std = series.std()\n    z_scores = np.abs((series - mean) / std)\n    return z_scores > threshold\n\n# Apply to daily sales\nanomalies = detect_anomalies_zscore(daily_sales)\n```\n\n#### IQR Method\n```python\ndef detect_anomalies_iqr(series, k=1.5):\n    \"\"\"Detect anomalies using Interquartile Range\"\"\"\n    Q1 = series.quantile(0.25)\n    Q3 = series.quantile(0.75)\n    IQR = Q3 - Q1\n    lower_bound = Q1 - k * IQR\n    upper_bound = Q3 + k * IQR\n    return (series < lower_bound) | (series > upper_bound)\n\nanomalies = detect_anomalies_iqr(daily_sales, k=1.5)\n```\n\n### 3. Counterfactual Prediction\n\n#### Time Series Decomposition\n```python\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.seasonal import seasonal_decompose\n\n# Decompose into trend, seasonal, and residual components\ndecomposition = seasonal_decompose(historical_sales, model='additive', period=365)\ntrend = decomposition.trend\nseasonal = decomposition.seasonal\nresidual = decomposition.resid\n\n# Predict March 2020 counterfactual (what would have happened)\n# assuming no treatment effect\npredicted_march = trend.iloc[-1] + seasonal['2020-03']\n```\n\n#### ARIMA Method\n```python\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.arima.model import ARIMA\n\n# Fit ARIMA on historical data\nmodel = ARIMA(historical_sales, order=(5, 1, 2))\nresults = model.fit()\n\n# Forecast March 2020\nforecast = results.get_forecast(steps=31)\npredicted_march = forecast.predicted_mean\nconfidence_intervals = forecast.conf_int()\n```\n\n### 4. Deviation Index Calculation\n\n```python\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(observed, predicted, tolerance=0.1):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly index from -100 to 100\n    100 = very unusual increase\n    0 = normal\n    -100 = unusual decrease\n    \"\"\"\n    pct_change = (observed - predicted) / (predicted + 1e-6)\n\n    if pct_change > 0:\n        # Surge: scale positive deviations to 0-100\n        anomaly_index = min(100, (pct_change / (pct_change + tolerance)) * 100)\n    else:\n        # Slump: scale negative deviations to -100-0\n        anomaly_index = max(-100, (pct_change / (abs(pct_change) + tolerance)) * 100)\n\n    return anomaly_index\n\n# Apply to all categories\ncategory_anomalies = {}\nfor category in categories:\n    observed = march_sales[category].sum()\n    predicted = counterfactual[category]\n    anomaly_index = calculate_anomaly_index(observed, predicted)\n    category_anomalies[category] = anomaly_index\n```\n\n### 5. Multi-Step Anomaly Pipeline\n\n```python\ndef detect_category_anomalies(daily_sales_df, target_period='2020-03'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete pipeline for category-level anomaly detection\n\n    daily_sales_df: DataFrame with columns [date, category, sales]\n    target_period: str in 'YYYY-MM' format\n    \"\"\"\n    anomalies = {}\n\n    for category in daily_sales_df['category'].unique():\n        # Filter historical data (before target period)\n        category_data = daily_sales_df[daily_sales_df['category'] == category].copy()\n        category_data['date'] = pd.to_datetime(category_data['date'])\n\n        baseline = category_data[category_data['date'] < f'{target_period}-01']\n        target = category_data[category_data['date'].dt.to_period('M') == target_period]\n\n        # Estimate counterfactual for target period\n        # Method 1: Average of baseline period\n        counterfactual_mean = baseline['sales'].mean()\n\n        # Method 2: Seasonal adjustment\n        baseline['month'] = baseline['date'].dt.month\n        seasonal_factor = baseline[baseline['month'] == 3]['sales'].mean() / baseline['sales'].mean()\n        counterfactual_seasonal = counterfactual_mean * seasonal_factor\n\n        # Calculate actual vs predicted\n        actual_sales = target['sales'].sum()\n        predicted_sales = counterfactual_seasonal * len(target)\n\n        # Compute anomaly index\n        anomaly_idx = calculate_anomaly_index(actual_sales, predicted_sales)\n        anomalies[category] = {\n            'anomaly_index': anomaly_idx,\n            'actual': actual_sales,\n            'predicted': predicted_sales,\n            'n_days': len(target)\n        }\n\n    return anomalies\n```\n\n### 6. Robustness Checks\n\n```python\ndef validate_anomalies(daily_sales_df, anomalies):\n    \"\"\"Validate anomaly detection results\"\"\"\n\n    results = []\n    for category, metrics in anomalies.items():\n        # Check if anomaly is statistically significant\n        category_data = daily_sales_df[daily_sales_df['category'] == category]\n        baseline_std = category_data['sales'].std()\n\n        deviation = abs(metrics['actual'] - metrics['predicted'])\n        z_score = deviation / (baseline_std + 1e-6)\n\n        significant = z_score > 2.0  # 95% confidence\n\n        results.append({\n            'category': category,\n            'anomaly_index': metrics['anomaly_index'],\n            'z_score': z_score,\n            'significant': significant\n        })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n## Practical Example: E-Commerce Category Analysis\n\n```python\n# Load transaction data\ntransactions = pd.read_csv('transactions.csv')\ntransactions['date'] = pd.to_datetime(transactions['date'])\ntransactions['category'] = transactions['category'].str.lower().str.strip()\n\n# Create daily sales by category\ndaily_sales = transactions.groupby(['date', 'category']).agg({\n    'amount': 'sum',\n    'order_id': 'count'\n}).reset_index()\ndaily_sales.columns = ['date', 'category', 'sales', 'orders']\n\n# Detect anomalies in March 2020\nanomalies = detect_category_anomalies(daily_sales, target_period='2020-03')\n\n# Convert to DataFrame and sort by anomaly index\nanomaly_df = pd.DataFrame([\n    {'category': cat, 'anomaly_index': metrics['anomaly_index']}\n    for cat, metrics in anomalies.items()\n]).sort_values('anomaly_index', ascending=False)\n\n# Top 10 surges\ntop_surges = anomaly_df[anomaly_df['anomaly_index'] > 0].head(10)\n\n# Top 10 slumps\ntop_slumps = anomaly_df[anomaly_df['anomaly_index'] < 0].tail(10).sort_values('anomaly_index')\n\nprint(top_surges)\nprint(top_slumps)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- **Use multiple methods**: Combine ARIMA, LOESS, and seasonal decomposition\n- **Account for seasonality**: E-commerce has strong seasonal patterns\n- **Consider external events**: Document holidays, promotions, etc.\n- **Validate predictions**: Compare forecasts with hold-out test periods\n- **Sensitivity analysis**: Test with different parameters and thresholds\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Features should represent demographic characteristics, preferences, and behaviors.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy scikit-learn category_encoders\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### 1. Categorical Encoding Strategies\n\n#### One-Hot Encoding\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Simple one-hot encoding\nsurvey = pd.read_csv('survey.csv')\nsurvey_encoded = pd.get_dummies(survey, columns=['region', 'device_type'])\n\n# Specify drop parameter to avoid collinearity\nsurvey_encoded = pd.get_dummies(\n    survey,\n    columns=['region', 'device_type'],\n    drop_first=True\n)\n\n# Custom prefix\nsurvey_encoded = pd.get_dummies(\n    survey,\n    columns=['region'],\n    prefix='region'\n)\n```\n\n#### Ordinal Encoding (for ranked categories)\n```python\n# Map ordinal values (e.g., education level)\neducation_map = {\n    'High School': 1,\n    'Bachelor': 2,\n    'Master': 3,\n    'PhD': 4\n}\nsurvey['education_level'] = survey['education'].map(education_map)\n```\n\n#### Target Encoding\n```python\n# Encode categories by target variable mean (use cautiously)\nspending_by_category = transactions.groupby('customer_region')['spend'].mean()\nsurvey['region_avg_spend'] = survey['region'].map(spending_by_category)\n```\n\n### 2. Numerical Feature Engineering\n\n#### Scaling and Normalization\n```python\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler, MinMaxScaler\n\n# Standardization (mean=0, std=1)\nscaler = StandardScaler()\nsurvey['income_scaled'] = scaler.fit_transform(survey[['income']])\n\n# Min-Max scaling (0-1 range)\nminmax = MinMaxScaler()\nsurvey['age_normalized'] = minmax.fit_transform(survey[['age']])\n\n# Robust scaling (uses median and IQR, good for outliers)\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import RobustScaler\nrobust = RobustScaler()\nsurvey['purchase_freq_robust'] = robust.fit_transform(survey[['purchase_frequency']])\n```\n\n#### Binning and Discretization\n```python\n# Create income brackets\nsurvey['income_bracket'] = pd.cut(\n    survey['income'],\n    bins=[0, 30000, 60000, 100000, float('inf')],\n    labels=['Low', 'Medium', 'High', 'Very High']\n)\n\n# Quantile-based binning\nsurvey['age_quartile'] = pd.qcut(survey['age'], q=4, labels=['Q1', 'Q2', 'Q3', 'Q4'])\n```\n\n#### Log Transformation\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Transform skewed distributions\nsurvey['log_income'] = np.log1p(survey['income'])  # log1p handles zeros\nsurvey['log_purchase_value'] = np.log1p(survey['total_purchase_value'])\n```\n\n### 3. Interaction Features\n\n```python\n# Create interaction terms\nsurvey['high_income_young'] = (survey['income'] > survey['income'].median()) & (survey['age'] < 30)\nsurvey['income_x_age'] = survey['income'] * survey['age']\n\n# Higher-order interactions\nsurvey['income_age_interaction'] = (\n    (survey['income'] - survey['income'].mean()) *\n    (survey['age'] - survey['age'].mean())\n) / (survey['income'].std() * survey['age'].std())\n```\n\n### 4. Aggregation-Based Features\n\n```python\n# Aggregate purchase behavior at customer level\ncustomer_agg = transactions.groupby('customer_id').agg({\n    'order_id': 'count',\n    'amount': ['sum', 'mean', 'std'],\n    'date': [lambda x: (x.max() - x.min()).days, 'nunique']\n}).reset_index()\n\ncustomer_agg.columns = [\n    'customer_id',\n    'total_purchases',\n    'total_spend',\n    'avg_spend',\n    'spend_volatility',\n    'customer_lifetime_days',\n    'unique_days'\n]\n\n# Merge back to survey\nsurvey = survey.merge(customer_agg, left_on='customer_id', right_on='customer_id', how='left')\n```\n\n### 5. Temporal Features\n\n```python\n# Customer tenure\nsurvey['date_joined'] = pd.to_datetime(survey['date_joined'])\nreference_date = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\nsurvey['tenure_days'] = (reference_date - survey['date_joined']).dt.days\nsurvey['tenure_months'] = survey['tenure_days'] / 30\n```\n\n### 7. Behavioral Indicators\n\n```python\n# Purchase propensity\ncustomer_purchase_counts = transactions.groupby('customer_id').size()\nsurvey['is_active_purchaser'] = survey['customer_id'].isin(\n    customer_purchase_counts[customer_purchase_counts > 0].index\n).astype(int)\n\n# Category diversity\ncustomer_category_count = transactions.groupby('customer_id')['category'].nunique()\nsurvey['category_diversity'] = survey['customer_id'].map(customer_category_count).fillna(0)\n```\n\n## Complete Feature Engineering Pipeline\n\n```python\ndef engineer_survey_features(survey_df, transactions_df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete feature engineering pipeline\n\n    Returns feature-engineered survey with demographic and behavioral features\n    \"\"\"\n    survey = survey_df.copy()\n    transactions = transactions_df.copy()\n\n    # 1. Clean categorical variables\n    for col in survey.select_dtypes(include=['object']).columns:\n        survey[col] = survey[col].str.strip().str.lower()\n\n    # 2. Encode categorical features\n    categorical_cols = ['region', 'device_type', 'customer_segment']\n    survey = pd.get_dummies(survey, columns=categorical_cols, drop_first=True)\n\n    # 3. Create demographic brackets\n    survey['age_bracket'] = pd.cut(survey['age'], bins=[0, 25, 35, 50, 65, 120])\n    survey['income_bracket'] = pd.cut(survey['income'], bins=[0, 40000, 75000, 120000, float('inf')])\n    survey = pd.get_dummies(survey, columns=['age_bracket', 'income_bracket'])\n\n    # 4. Aggregate purchase behavior\n    customer_behavior = transactions.groupby('customer_id').agg({\n        'order_id': 'count',\n        'amount': ['sum', 'mean'],\n        'category': 'nunique'\n    }).reset_index()\n    customer_behavior.columns = ['customer_id', 'total_orders', 'total_spend', 'avg_order_value', 'num_categories']\n\n    survey = survey.merge(customer_behavior, on='customer_id', how='left')\n    survey[['total_orders', 'total_spend', 'avg_order_value', 'num_categories']] = \\\n        survey[['total_orders', 'total_spend', 'avg_order_value', 'num_categories']].fillna(0)\n\n    # 5. Scale numerical features\n    from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\n    scaler = StandardScaler()\n    numerical_cols = ['age', 'income', 'total_orders', 'total_spend', 'avg_order_value', 'num_categories']\n    survey_scaled = survey.copy()\n    survey_scaled[numerical_cols] = scaler.fit_transform(survey[numerical_cols])\n\n    # 6. Create interaction features\n    survey_scaled['high_income_active'] = (\n        (survey['income'] > survey['income'].quantile(0.75)) &\n        (survey['total_orders'] > 0)\n    ).astype(int)\n\n    return survey_scaled\n\n# Usage\nsurvey_engineered = engineer_survey_features(survey, transactions)\n```\n\n## Feature Selection for Causal Analysis\n\n```python\ndef select_features_for_causal_analysis(engineered_survey, max_features=30):\n    \"\"\"\n    Select most important features for causal analysis using correlation\n    \"\"\"\n    # Remove ID columns and target variables\n    exclude_cols = ['customer_id', 'survey_response_id', 'date']\n    feature_cols = [col for col in engineered_survey.columns\n                    if col not in exclude_cols and engineered_survey[col].dtype != 'object']\n\n    # Calculate feature variance (low variance = not useful)\n    variances = engineered_survey[feature_cols].var()\n    important_features = variances[variances > 0].index.tolist()\n\n    # Limit to top features by variance\n    important_features = sorted(\n        important_features,\n        key=lambda x: variances[x],\n        reverse=True\n    )[:max_features]\n\n    return important_features\n\n# Usage\nfeatures_for_analysis = select_features_for_causal_analysis(survey_engineered)\nprint(f\"Selected {len(features_for_analysis)} features for analysis\")\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- **Avoid data leakage**: Don't use post-treatment information in features\n- **Scale consistently**: Apply same scaling to train and test data\n- **Handle missing values**: Decide before encoding (drop, fill, or flag)\n- **Document transformations**: Record all feature engineering steps\n- **Multicollinearity check**: Avoid redundant or highly correlated features\n- **Interpretability**: Create features that have clear business meaning\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2305,"cache_read_input_tokens":22070,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2305,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8a8a691c-7be5-4ec3-80a4-0e949fdfd368","uuid":"eeb100c1-887a-46eb-908a-c26da04466bc"}

Behavioral Indicators\n\n```python\n# Purchase propensity\ncustomer_purchase_counts = transactions.groupby('customer_id').size()\nsurvey['is_active_purchaser'] = survey['customer_id'].isin(\n    customer_purchase_counts[customer_purchase_counts > 0].index\n).astype(int)\n\n# Category diversity\ncustomer_category_count = transactions.groupby('customer_id')['category'].nunique()\nsurvey['category_diversity'] = survey['customer_id'].map(customer_category_count).fillna(0)\n```\n\n## Complete Feature Engineering Pipeline\n\n```python\ndef engineer_survey_features(survey_df, transactions_df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete feature engineering pipeline\n\n    Returns feature-engineered survey with demographic and behavioral features\n    \"\"\"\n    survey = survey_df.copy()\n    transactions = transactions_df.copy()\n\n    # 1. Clean categorical variables\n    for col in survey.select_dtypes(include=['object']).columns:\n        survey[col] = survey[col].str.strip().str.lower()\n\n    # 2. Encode categorical features\n    categorical_cols = ['region', 'device_type', 'customer_segment']\n    survey = pd.get_dummies(survey, columns=categorical_cols, drop_first=True)\n\n    # 3. Create demographic brackets\n    survey['age_bracket'] = pd.cut(survey['age'], bins=[0, 25, 35, 50, 65, 120])\n    survey['income_bracket'] = pd.cut(survey['income'], bins=[0, 40000, 75000, 120000, float('inf')])\n    survey = pd.get_dummies(survey, columns=['age_bracket', 'income_bracket'])\n\n    # 4. Aggregate purchase behavior\n    customer_behavior = transactions.groupby('customer_id').agg({\n        'order_id': 'count',\n        'amount': ['sum', 'mean'],\n        'category': 'nunique'\n    }).reset_index()\n    customer_behavior.columns = ['customer_id', 'total_orders', 'total_spend', 'avg_order_value', 'num_categories']\n\n    survey = survey.merge(customer_behavior, on='customer_id', how='left')\n    survey[['total_orders', 'total_spend', 'avg_order_value', 'num_categories']] = \\\n        survey[['total_orders', 'total_spend', 'avg_order_value', 'num_categories']].fillna(0)\n\n    # 5. Scale numerical features\n    from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\n    scaler = StandardScaler()\n    numerical_cols = ['age', 'income', 'total_orders', 'total_spend', 'avg_order_value', 'num_categories']\n    survey_scaled = survey.copy()\n    survey_scaled[numerical_cols] = scaler.fit_transform(survey[numerical_cols])\n\n    # 6. Create interaction features\n    survey_scaled['high_income_active'] = (\n        (survey['income'] > survey['income'].quantile(0.75)) &\n        (survey['total_orders'] > 0)\n    ).astype(int)\n\n    return survey_scaled\n\n# Usage\nsurvey_engineered = engineer_survey_features(survey, transactions)\n```\n\n## Feature Selection for Causal Analysis\n\n```python\ndef select_features_for_causal_analysis(engineered_survey, max_features=30):\n    \"\"\"\n    Select most important features for causal analysis using correlation\n    \"\"\"\n    # Remove ID columns and target variables\n    exclude_cols = ['customer_id', 'survey_response_id', 'date']\n    feature_cols = [col for col in engineered_survey.columns\n                    if col not in exclude_cols and engineered_survey[col].dtype != 'object']\n\n    # Calculate feature variance (low variance = not useful)\n    variances = engineered_survey[feature_cols].var()\n    important_features = variances[variances > 0].index.tolist()\n\n    # Limit to top features by variance\n    important_features = sorted(\n        important_features,\n        key=lambda x: variances[x],\n        reverse=True\n    )[:max_features]\n\n    return important_features\n\n# Usage\nfeatures_for_analysis = select_features_for_causal_analysis(survey_engineered)\nprint(f\"Selected {len(features_for_analysis)} features for analysis\")\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- **Avoid data leakage**: Don't use post-treatment information in features\n- **Scale consistently**: Apply same scaling to train and test data\n- **Handle missing values**: Decide before encoding (drop, fill, or flag)\n- **Document transformations**: Record all feature engineering steps\n- **Multicollinearity check**: Avoid redundant or highly correlated features\n- **Interpretability**: Create features that have clear business meaning\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01NMea9kx6SXe4t8yL2U82xX","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01Cg98EtxFp9oZUGXH4fJ3Bi","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/environment/skills/difference-in-differences-analysis.md","content":"# Difference-in-Differences (DiD) Causal Analysis\n\n## Overview\nDifference-in-Differences is a quasi-experimental design technique used to estimate causal effects by comparing changes over time between a treatment group and control group. Essential for understanding what demographic factors drove March 2020 spending anomalies.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy scipy statsmodels scikit-learn\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### 1. Basic DiD Framework\n\nDiD estimates the causal effect by exploiting variation in treatment timing:\n\n```\nEffect = (Y_treatment,post - Y_treatment,pre) - (Y_control,post - Y_control,pre)\n```\n\n- **Treatment period**: March 2020 (when anomalies occur)\n- **Baseline period**: January-February 2020\n- **Treatment group**: Customers in surge/slump categories\n- **Control groups**: Customers in other categories\n\n### 2. Univariate DiD Estimation\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy import stats\nfrom statsmodels.stats.outliers_influence import variance_inflation_factor\n\ndef univariate_did(df, outcome_col, feature_col, baseline_period, treatment_period):\n    \"\"\"\n    Univariate DiD: Estimate effect of one feature at a time\n\n    Parameters:\n    -----------\n    df : DataFrame with columns ['period', 'feature', 'outcome', 'category']\n    outcome_col : str, column name for outcome variable (e.g., 'Total_Spend')\n    feature_col : str, column name for feature variable\n    baseline_period : str, period label (e.g., 'baseline')\n    treatment_period : str, period label (e.g., 'treatment')\n\n    Returns:\n    --------\n    dict with DiD estimate, p-value, and statistics\n    \"\"\"\n    # Create treatment indicator (feature value > median)\n    feature_median = df[feature_col].median()\n    df = df.copy()\n    df['treated'] = (df[feature_col] > feature_median).astype(int)\n    df['post'] = (df['period'] == treatment_period).astype(int)\n\n    # DiD interaction\n    df['did_interaction'] = df['treated'] * df['post']\n\n    # Run regression: y = β0 + β1(treated) + β2(post) + β3(interaction) + ε\n    from statsmodels.formula.api import ols\n    from statsmodels.stats.anova import anova_lm\n\n    formula = f'{outcome_col} ~ C(treated) + C(post) + did_interaction'\n    model = ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n    # Extract DiD coefficient and p-value\n    did_estimate = model.params['did_interaction']\n    did_pvalue = model.pvalues['did_interaction']\n\n    return {\n        'feature': feature_col,\n        'did_estimate': did_estimate,\n        'p_value': did_pvalue,\n        'model_r_squared': model.rsquared,\n        'method': 'Univariate DiD'\n    }\n\n# Usage\nbaseline = 'baseline'\ntreatment = 'treatment'\n\nresults = []\nfor feature in engineered_features:\n    result = univariate_did(did_data, 'Total_Spend', feature, baseline, treatment)\n    results.append(result)\n\nresults_df = pd.DataFrame(results).sort_values('did_estimate', ascending=False)\n```\n\n### 3. Multivariate DiD with Heterogeneous Effects\n\n```python\ndef multivariate_heterogeneous_did(df, outcome_col, feature_cols, baseline_period, treatment_period):\n    \"\"\"\n    Multivariate DiD: Estimate heterogeneous effects controlling for multiple features\n\n    For each feature, estimate how that feature moderates the treatment effect,\n    holding other features constant at their medians\n    \"\"\"\n    from statsmodels.formula.api import ols\n\n    results = []\n    df = df.copy()\n\n    # Standardize features for interpretation\n    for col in feature_cols:\n        df[f'{col}_std'] = (df[col] - df[col].mean()) / df[col].std()\n\n    df['post'] = (df['period'] == treatment_period).astype(int)\n\n    for feature in feature_cols:\n        # Create interaction: (feature interaction with post indicator)\n        df['feature_x_post'] = df[f'{feature}_std'] * df['post']\n\n        # Full model with all features and their post interactions\n        formula_parts = ['C(post)']\n        for f in feature_cols:\n            formula_parts.append(f'C({f}_std)')\n            formula_parts.append(f'{f}_std:C(post)')\n\n        formula = f'{outcome_col} ~ {\" + \".join(formula_parts)}'\n\n        try:\n            model = ols(formula, data=df).fit()\n\n            # The coefficient for feature_x_post is the heterogeneous effect\n            feature_interaction_col = f'{feature}_std:C(post)[T.True]'\n            if feature_interaction_col in model.params.index:\n                did_estimate = model.params[feature_interaction_col]\n                did_pvalue = model.pvalues[feature_interaction_col]\n            else:\n                # Try alternative naming\n                feature_interaction_col = f'{feature}_std:post'\n                did_estimate = model.params.get(feature_interaction_col, np.nan)\n                did_pvalue = model.pvalues.get(feature_interaction_col, np.nan)\n\n            results.append({\n                'feature': feature,\n                'did_estimate': did_estimate,\n                'p_value': did_pvalue,\n                'method': 'Multivariate Heterogeneous DiD'\n            })\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error for feature {feature}: {e}\")\n            continue\n\n    return results\n```\n\n### 4. Intensive vs. Extensive Margin Analysis\n\n```python\ndef analyze_intensive_margin(df, baseline_period, treatment_period):\n    \"\"\"\n    Intensive margin: Change in spending AMONG PURCHASERS\n    (excluding non-purchasers)\n\n    Outcome: Total_Spend | among those who made purchases\n    \"\"\"\n    # Filter to customers who purchased in the target category\n    df_intensive = df[df['Total_Spend'] > 0].copy()\n\n    results = []\n    for feature in feature_cols:\n        result = univariate_did(df_intensive, 'Total_Spend', feature, baseline_period, treatment_period)\n        results.append(result)\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results).sort_values('did_estimate', ascending=False).head(3)\n\ndef analyze_extensive_margin(df, baseline_period, treatment_period):\n    \"\"\"\n    Extensive margin: Change in PROBABILITY OF PURCHASE\n    (whether customers purchase at all)\n\n    Outcome: Has_Purchase (binary indicator, 1 if any purchase in period)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Create purchase indicator\n    df = df.copy()\n    df['Has_Purchase'] = (df['Total_Spend'] > 0).astype(int)\n\n    results = []\n    for feature in feature_cols:\n        result = univariate_did(df, 'Has_Purchase', feature, baseline_period, treatment_period)\n        results.append(result)\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results).sort_values('did_estimate', ascending=False).head(3)\n```\n\n### 5. Parallel Trends Assumption Check\n\n```python\ndef check_parallel_trends(df, baseline_period, treatment_period):\n    \"\"\"\n    Verify that treatment and control groups had parallel trends\n    in the pre-period (baseline).\n\n    This is a critical assumption for DiD validity.\n    \"\"\"\n    baseline_data = df[df['period'] == baseline_period].copy()\n\n    # For each month in baseline, compare treated vs control trends\n    treated_median = df['feature'].median()\n    baseline_data['treated'] = (baseline_data['feature'] > treated_median).astype(int)\n\n    # Analyze trends\n    trends = baseline_data.groupby(['month', 'treated'])['outcome'].mean().unstack()\n\n    # Calculate slope for each group\n    treated_slope = trends[1].values\n    control_slope = trends[0].values\n\n    # Test if slopes are significantly different\n    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind\n    t_stat, p_value = ttest_ind(treated_slope, control_slope)\n\n    return {\n        'trends_parallel': p_value > 0.05,\n        'p_value': p_value,\n        'treated_avg_trend': treated_slope.mean(),\n        'control_avg_trend': control_slope.mean()\n    }\n```\n\n### 6. Complete DiD Pipeline\n\n```python\ndef run_complete_did_analysis(transactions, survey, category_name, baseline_start, baseline_end, treatment_start, treatment_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete DiD analysis pipeline for a single category\n\n    Returns intensive and extensive margin effects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Merge demographic and transaction data\n    merged = transactions.merge(survey[['customer_id', 'feature1', 'feature2', ...]], on='customer_id')\n\n    # Filter to target category\n    merged = merged[merged['category'] == category_name].copy()\n\n    # Create period indicators\n    merged['date'] = pd.to_datetime(merged['date'])\n    merged['period'] = 'baseline'\n    merged.loc[merged['date'] >= treatment_start, 'period'] = 'treatment'\n\n    # Aggregate to user-period level for analysis\n    did_data = merged.groupby(['customer_id', 'period']).agg({\n        'amount': 'sum',\n        'order_id': 'count',\n        'feature1': 'first',\n        'feature2': 'first'\n    }).reset_index()\n    did_data.columns = ['customer_id', 'period', 'Total_Spend', 'order_count', 'feature1', 'feature2']\n\n    # Intensive margin analysis\n    intensive_results = analyze_intensive_margin(did_data, 'baseline', 'treatment')\n\n    # Extensive margin analysis\n    extensive_results = analyze_extensive_margin(did_data, 'baseline', 'treatment')\n\n    return {\n        'category': category_name,\n        'intensive_margin': intensive_results.to_dict('records'),\n        'extensive_margin': extensive_results.to_dict('records')\n    }\n```\n\n## Practical Example: Category Analysis\n\n```python\n# Prepare data for DiD analysis\ncategory_data = transactions.merge(\n    survey[['customer_id', 'age', 'income', 'region', 'device_type']],\n    on='customer_id'\n)\n\n# Define time periods\nbaseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\nbaseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\ntreatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\ntreatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n# Create aggregated dataset for DiD\nbaseline_agg = category_data[\n    (category_data['date'] >= baseline_start) &\n    (category_data['date'] <= baseline_end)\n].groupby(['customer_id', 'category']).agg({\n    'amount': 'sum',\n    'order_id': 'count',\n    'age': 'first',\n    'income': 'first',\n}).reset_index()\nbaseline_agg['period'] = 'baseline'\n\ntreatment_agg = category_data[\n    (category_data['date'] >= treatment_start) &\n    (category_data['date'] <= treatment_end)\n].groupby(['customer_id', 'category']).agg({\n    'amount': 'sum',\n    'order_id': 'count',\n    'age': 'first',\n    'income': 'first',\n}).reset_index()\ntreatment_agg['period'] = 'treatment'\n\n# Combine\ndid_data = pd.concat([baseline_agg, treatment_agg], ignore_index=True)\n\n# Run analysis\nresults = []\nfor surge_category in top_surge_categories:\n    category_results = run_complete_did_analysis(\n        did_data,\n        surge_category,\n        baseline_start,\n        baseline_end,\n        treatment_start,\n        treatment_end\n    )\n    results.append(category_results)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- **Check parallel trends**: Verify assumption before interpreting results\n- **Report confidence intervals**: Not just point estimates\n- **Consider alternative specifications**: Test robustness\n- **Document data preparation**: Track filters and aggregation choices\n- **Control for confounders**: Include relevant demographics in multivariate models\n- **Report sample sizes**: Show how many customers in each group\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2735,"cache_read_input_tokens":24375,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2735,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8a8a691c-7be5-4ec3-80a4-0e949fdfd368","uuid":"bfdb2bdd-b7e0-4028-987c-a1873cd4b1ba"}

Complete DiD Pipeline\n\n```python\ndef run_complete_did_analysis(transactions, survey, category_name, baseline_start, baseline_end, treatment_start, treatment_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete DiD analysis pipeline for a single category\n\n    Returns intensive and extensive margin effects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Merge demographic and transaction data\n    merged = transactions.merge(survey[['customer_id', 'feature1', 'feature2', ...]], on='customer_id')\n\n    # Filter to target category\n    merged = merged[merged['category'] == category_name].copy()\n\n    # Create period indicators\n    merged['date'] = pd.to_datetime(merged['date'])\n    merged['period'] = 'baseline'\n    merged.loc[merged['date'] >= treatment_start, 'period'] = 'treatment'\n\n    # Aggregate to user-period level for analysis\n    did_data = merged.groupby(['customer_id', 'period']).agg({\n        'amount': 'sum',\n        'order_id': 'count',\n        'feature1': 'first',\n        'feature2': 'first'\n    }).reset_index()\n    did_data.columns = ['customer_id', 'period', 'Total_Spend', 'order_count', 'feature1', 'feature2']\n\n    # Intensive margin analysis\n    intensive_results = analyze_intensive_margin(did_data, 'baseline', 'treatment')\n\n    # Extensive margin analysis\n    extensive_results = analyze_extensive_margin(did_data, 'baseline', 'treatment')\n\n    return {\n        'category': category_name,\n        'intensive_margin': intensive_results.to_dict('records'),\n        'extensive_margin': extensive_results.to_dict('records')\n    }\n```\n\n## Practical Example: Category Analysis\n\n```python\n# Prepare data for DiD analysis\ncategory_data = transactions.merge(\n    survey[['customer_id', 'age', 'income', 'region', 'device_type']],\n    on='customer_id'\n)\n\n# Define time periods\nbaseline_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-01-01')\nbaseline_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-02-29')\ntreatment_start = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-01')\ntreatment_end = pd.to_datetime('2020-03-31')\n\n# Create aggregated dataset for DiD\nbaseline_agg = category_data[\n    (category_data['date'] >= baseline_start) &\n    (category_data['date'] <= baseline_end)\n].groupby(['customer_id', 'category']).agg({\n    'amount': 'sum',\n    'order_id': 'count',\n    'age': 'first',\n    'income': 'first',\n}).reset_index()\nbaseline_agg['period'] = 'baseline'\n\ntreatment_agg = category_data[\n    (category_data['date'] >= treatment_start) &\n    (category_data['date'] <= treatment_end)\n].groupby(['customer_id', 'category']).agg({\n    'amount': 'sum',\n    'order_id': 'count',\n    'age': 'first',\n    'income': 'first',\n}).reset_index()\ntreatment_agg['period'] = 'treatment'\n\n# Combine\ndid_data = pd.concat([baseline_agg, treatment_agg], ignore_index=True)\n\n# Run analysis\nresults = []\nfor surge_category in top_surge_categories:\n    category_results = run_complete_did_analysis(\n        did_data,\n        surge_category,\n        baseline_start,\n        baseline_end,\n        treatment_start,\n        treatment_end\n    )\n    results.append(category_results)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n- **Check parallel trends**: Verify assumption before interpreting results\n- **Report confidence intervals**: Not just point estimates\n- Essential for sales trend analysis.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### 1. Basic Time-Based Grouping\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Create sample transaction data\ntransactions = pd.read_csv('transactions.csv')\ntransactions['date'] = pd.to_datetime(transactions['date'])\n\n# Daily aggregation\ndaily_sales = transactions.groupby(\n    transactions['date'].dt.date\n)['amount'].sum().reset_index()\ndaily_sales.columns = ['date', 'total_sales']\n\n# Weekly aggregation\nweekly_sales = transactions.set_index('date').resample('W')['amount'].sum()\n\n# Monthly aggregation\nmonthly_sales = transactions.set_index('date').resample('M')['amount'].sum()\n```\n\n### 2. Multi-Level Grouping\n\n```python\n# Daily sales by category\ndaily_category = transactions.groupby([\n    transactions['date'].dt.date,\n    'category'\n])['amount'].agg(['sum', 'count', 'mean']).reset_index()\n\n# Customer-level aggregations\ncustomer_summary = transactions.groupby('customer_id').agg({\n    'amount': ['sum', 'mean', 'count', 'std'],\n    'date': ['min', 'max', lambda x: (x.max() - x.min()).days],\n    'category': 'nunique'\n}).reset_index()\n\n# Flatten column names after aggregation\ncustomer_summary.columns = ['_'.join(col).strip('_') for col in customer_summary.columns.values]\n```\n\n### 3. Pivot Tables for Dimension Analysis\n\n```python\n# Create pivot table: rows=date, columns=category, values=sales\npivot_by_category = transactions.pivot_table(\n    index=transactions['date'].dt.date,\n    columns='category',\n    values='amount',\n    aggfunc='sum',\n    fill_value=0\n)\n\n# Multiple aggregations in pivot table\npivot_detailed = transactions.pivot_table(\n    index=transactions['date'].dt.date,\n    columns='category',\n    values=['amount', 'order_id'],\n    aggfunc={'amount': 'sum', 'order_id': 'count'},\n    fill_value=0\n)\n```\n\n### 4. Resampling for Regular Time Series\n\n```python\n# Create complete date range (filling missing dates with 0)\ndaily_sales = transactions.set_index('date').resample('D').agg({\n    'amount': 'sum',\n    'order_id': 'count'\n}).reset_index()\ndaily_sales.columns = ['date', 'total_sales', 'num_orders']\n\n# Forward fill missing values (carry forward last value)\ndaily_sales['sales_filled'] = daily_sales['total_sales'].fillna(method='ffill')\n\n# Backward fill\ndaily_sales['sales_bfill'] = daily_sales['total_sales'].fillna(method='bfill')\n\n# Interpolate (linear between values)\ndaily_sales['sales_interpolated'] = daily_sales['total_sales'].interpolate()\n```\n\n### 5. Rolling Window Calculations\n\n```python\n# 7-day moving average\ntransactions_sorted = transactions.sort_values('date')\ntransactions_sorted['sales_7day_ma'] = transactions_sorted['amount'].rolling(\n    window=7,\n    min_periods=1\n).mean()\n\n# Rolling sum (total in last 30 days)\ntransactions_sorted['sales_30day_sum'] = transactions_sorted['amount'].rolling(\n    window=30,\n    min_periods=1\n).sum()\n\n# Expanding window (cumulative)\ntransactions_sorted['cumulative_sales'] = transactions_sorted['amount'].expanding().sum()\n```\n\n### 6. Time-Based Filtering\n\n```python\n# Filter to specific date range\nbaseline = transactions[\n    (transactions['date'] >= '2020-01-01') &\n    (transactions['date'] <= '2020-02-29')\n]\n\n# Extract components\ntransactions['year'] = transactions['date'].dt.year\ntransactions['month'] = transactions['date'].dt.month\ntransactions['day'] = transactions['date'].dt.day\ntransactions['quarter'] = transactions['date'].dt.quarter\ntransactions['week'] = transactions['date'].dt.isocalendar().week\ntransactions['day_of_week'] = transactions['date'].dt.dayofweek  # 0=Monday\n\n# Filter by temporal feature\nmarch_2020 = transactions[\n    (transactions['year'] == 2020) &\n    (transactions['month'] == 3)\n]\n```\n\n### 7. Period-Based Operations\n\n```python\n# Create period column for grouping\ntransactions['period'] = 'baseline'\ntransactions.loc[transactions['date'] >= '2020-03-01', 'period'] = 'treatment'\n\n# Aggregate by period and category\nperiod_category = transactions.groupby(['period', 'category']).agg({\n    'amount': ['sum', 'count', 'mean'],\n    'customer_id': 'nunique'\n}).reset_index()\n\n# Calculate period metrics\nbaseline_avg = transactions[transactions['period'] == 'baseline']['amount'].mean()\ntreatment_avg = transactions[transactions['period'] == 'treatment']['amount'].mean()\nperiod_pct_change = (treatment_avg - baseline_avg) / baseline_avg * 100\n```\n\n## Complete Time Aggregation Pipeline\n\n```python\ndef aggregate_sales_for_anomaly_detection(transactions_df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Aggregate transaction data into daily sales by category for anomaly detection\n\n    Returns DataFrame with [date, category, daily_sales, num_orders]\n    \"\"\"\n    df = transactions_df.copy()\n    df['date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['date'])\n    df['category'] = df['category'].str.lower().str.strip()\n\n    # Daily aggregation by category\n    daily = df.groupby([\n        df['date'].dt.date,\n        'category'\n    ]).agg({\n        'amount': 'sum',\n        'order_id': 'count'\n    }).reset_index()\n\n    daily.columns = ['date', 'category', 'daily_sales', 'num_orders']\n    daily['date'] = pd.to_datetime(daily['date'])\n\n    return daily\n\ndef prepare_did_data(transactions_df, survey_df, baseline_start, baseline_end, treatment_start, treatment_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Prepare aggregated data for Difference-in-Differences analysis\n\n    Returns: (intensive_agg, extensive_agg)\n    - intensive_agg: user-category-period spending (among purchasers)\n    - extensive_agg: user-category-period purchase indicator (all users)\n    \"\"\"\n    df = transactions_df.merge(survey_df, on='customer_id', how='inner')\n    df['date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['date'])\n\n    # Create period indicator\n    df['period'] = 'baseline'\n    df.loc[df['date'] >= treatment_start, 'period'] = 'treatment'\n\n    # Filter to analysis periods\n    df = df[\n        ((df['date'] >= baseline_start) & (df['date'] <= baseline_end)) |\n        ((df['date'] >= treatment_start) & (df['date'] <= treatment_end))\n    ].copy()\n\n    # Intensive margin: aggregate spending by user-category-period\n    intensive = df.groupby([\n        'customer_id',\n        'category',\n        'period'\n    ]).agg({\n        'amount': 'sum'\n    }).reset_index()\n    intensive.columns = ['customer_id', 'category', 'period', 'Total_Spend']\n\n    # Extensive margin: whether user purchased in category-period\n    extensive = df.groupby([\n        'customer_id',\n        'category',\n        'period'\n    ]).size().reset_index(name='transaction_count')\n    extensive['Has_Purchase'] = (extensive['transaction_count'] > 0).astype(int)\n    extensive = extensive[['customer_id', 'category', 'period', 'Has_Purchase']]\n\n    # Merge survey features for analysis\n    feature_cols = [col for col in survey_df.columns if col != 'customer_id']\n    intensive = intensive.merge(survey_df, on='customer_id', how='left')\n    extensive = extensive.merge(survey_df, on='customer_id', how='left')\n\n    return intensive, extensive\n\n# Usage\ndaily_sales = aggregate_sales_for_anomaly_detection(transactions)\nintensive_data, extensive_data = prepare_did_data(\n    transactions,\n    survey,\n    baseline_start='2020-01-01',\n    baseline_end='2020-02-29',\n    treatment_start='2020-03-01',\n    treatment_end='2020-03-31'\n)\n```\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n```python\n# transactions['category'].astype('category')\ntransactions['date'] = pd.to_datetime(transactions['date'])\n\n# This reduces memory usage significantly\nprint(f\"Memory usage: {transactions.memory_usage(deep=True).sum() / 1024**2:.2f} MB\")\n\n# Use groupby with observed=True for categorical data\ndaily = transactions.groupby(['date', 'category'], observed=True).agg({\n ============================================================================\n# STEP 2: DETECT ANOMALIES IN CATEGORY TRENDS\n# ============================================================================\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 80)\nprint(\"STEP 4: Detecting Category Anomalies\")\nprint(\"=\" * 80)\n\n# Create daily sales by category\ndaily_sales = transactions_filtered.copy()\ndaily_sales['Date'] = pd.to_datetime(daily_sales['Date'])\ndaily_sales = daily_sales.groupby([daily_sales['Date'].dt.date, 'Category']).agg({\n    'Amount': 'sum',\n    'Survey_ResponseID': 'count'\n}).reset_index()\ndaily_sales.columns = ['Date', 'Category', 'Daily_Sales', 'Num_Transactions']\ndaily_sales['Date'] = pd.to_datetime(daily_sales['Date'])\n\nprint(f\"\\nDaily sales records created: {len(daily_sales)}\")\nprint(f\"Date range: {daily_sales['Date'].min()} to {daily_sales['Date'].max()}\")\n\n# Calculate anomaly index for each category\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(observed, predicted):\n    \"\"\"Calculate anomaly index from -100 to 100\"\"\"\n    if predicted == 0:\n        predicted = 1e-6\n\n    pct_change = (observed - predicted) / abs(predicted)\n\n    if pct_change > 0:\n        # Surge: scale to 0-100\n        anomaly_index = min(100, (pct_change / (pct_change + 0.1)) * 100)\n    else:\n        # Slump: scale to -100-0\n        anomaly_index = max(-100, (pct_change / (abs(pct_change) + 0.1)) * 100)\n\n    return anomaly_index\n\n# Calculate anomalies\nanomalies = {}\ncategory_metrics = {}\n\nfor category in daily_sales['Category'].unique():\n    category_data = daily_sales[daily_sales['Category'] == category].copy()\n\n    # Separate baseline and treatment periods\n    baseline = category_data[category_data['Date'] < TREATMENT_START]\n    treatment = category_data[\n        (category_data['Date'] >= TREATMENT_START) &\n        (category_data['Date'] <= TREATMENT_END)\n    ]\n\n    if len(baseline) == 0 or len(treatment) == 0:\n        continue\n\n    # Calculate baseline statistics\n    baseline_mean = baseline['Daily_Sales'].mean()\n    baseline_std = baseline['Daily_Sales'].std()\n\n    # Actual March 2020 sales\n    actual_march_sales = treatment['Daily_Sales'].sum()\n\n    # Counterfactual: expected sales based on baseline average\n    predicted_march_sales = baseline_mean * len(treatment)\n\n    # Calculate anomaly index\n    anomaly_idx = calculate_anomaly_index(actual_march_sales, predicted_march_sales)\n    anomalies[category] = anomaly_idx\n\n    # Store metrics for later analysis\n    category_metrics[category] = {\n        'baseline_mean': baseline_mean,\n        'baseline_std': baseline_std,\n        'actual_march': actual_march_sales,\n        'predicted_march': predicted_march_sales,\n        'n_baseline_days': len(baseline),\n        'n_treatment_days': len(treatment)\n    }\n\n# Create anomaly dataframe\nanomaly_df = pd.DataFrame([\n    {'Category': cat, 'Anomaly_Index': idx}\n    for cat, idx in anomalies.items()\n]).sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False)\n\nprint(f\"\\nAnomalies detected for {len(anomaly_df)} categories\")\nprint(\"\\nTop 10 Surge Categories:\")\nprint(anomaly_df[anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'] > 0].head(10))\nprint(\"\\nTop 10 Slump Categories:\")\nprint(anomaly_df[anomaly_df['Anomaly_Index'] < 0].tail(10))\n\n# Save anomaly index\nanomaly_df.to_csv(f'{OUTPUT_DIR}/category_anomaly_index.csv', index=False)\n\n# Get top categories 0].tail(10)['Category'].tolist()\n\nprint(f\"\\nTop surge categories for analysis: {top_surge_categories}\")\nprint(f\"Top slump categories for analysis: {top_slump_categories}\")\n\n# ============================================================================\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 80)\nprint(\"STEP 5: Engineering Demographic Features\")\nprint(\"=\" * 80)\n\nsurvey_features = survey_clean.copy()\nprint(f\"\\nStarting with {len(survey_features)} survey responses\")\n\n# Rename ID column for consistency\nsurvey_features = survey_features.rename(columns={'Survey ResponseID': 'Survey_ResponseID'})\n\n# Process age\nif 'Q-demos-age' in survey_features.columns:\n    age_map = {\n        '18 - 24 years': 21,\n        '25 - 34 years': 29,\n        '35 - 44 years': 39,\n        '45 - 54 years': 49,\n        '55 - 64 years': 59,\n        '65 - 74 years': 69,\n        '75 - 84 years': 79,\n        '85 years or older': 85\n    }\n    survey_features['ENG_AGE_NUMERIC'] = survey_features['Q-demos-age'].map(age_map)\n    survey_features['ENG_AGE_NUMERIC'] = pd.to_numeric(survey_features['ENG_AGE_NUMERIC'], errors='coerce')\n\n# Process income\nif 'Q-demos-income' in survey_features.columns:\n    income_map = {\n        'Less than $25,000': 12500,\n        '$25,000 - $49,999': 37500,\n        '$50,000 - $74,999': 62500,\n        '$75,000 - $99,999': 87500,\n        '$100,000 - $124,999': 112500,\n        '$125,000 - $149,999': 137500,\n        '$150,000 or more': 175000\n    }\n    survey_features['ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC'] = survey_features['Q-demos-income'].str.strip().map(income_map)\n    survey_features['ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC'] = pd.to_numeric(survey_features['ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC'], errors='coerce')\n\n# Create binary demographic indicators\nsurvey_features['ENG_FEMALE'] = (survey_features.get('Q-demos-gender', '').str.lower() == 'female').astype(int)\n\nif 'Q-demos-education' in survey_features.columns:\n    survey_features['ENG_COLLEGE_EDU'] = survey_features['Q-demos-education'].str.contains(\n        'Bachelor|Master|Professional|Doctorate', case=False, na=False\n    ).astype(int)\n\nif 'Q-amazon-use-how-oft' in survey_features.columns:\n    frequent_use = survey_features['Q-amazon-use-how-oft'].str.lower().str.contains(\n        'daily|weekly|few times', na=False\n    )\n    survey_features['ENG_FREQUENT_AMAZON_USER'] = frequent_use.astype(int)\n\n# Merge purchase behavior into survey\ntransaction_summary = transactions_filtered.copy()\ntransaction_summary['Date'] = pd.to_datetime(transaction_summary['Date'])\n\ncustomer_behavior = transaction_summary.groupby('Survey_ResponseID').agg({\n    'Amount': ['sum', 'mean', 'count'],\n    'Category': 'nunique'\n}).reset_index()\ncustomer_behavior.columns = ['Survey_ResponseID', 'ENG_TOTAL_SPEND', 'ENG_AVG_ORDER_VALUE', 'ENG_NUM_ORDERS', 'ENG_NUM_CATEGORIES']\n\nsurvey_features = survey_features.merge(customer_behavior, on='Survey_ResponseID', how='left')\n\n# Fill missing behavior values with 0 (for non-purchasers)\nfor col in ['ENG_TOTAL_SPEND', 'ENG_AVG_ORDER_VALUE', 'ENG_NUM_ORDERS', 'ENG_NUM_CATEGORIES']:\n    if col in survey_features.columns:\n        survey_features[col] = survey_features[col].fillna(0)\n\n# Create indicator for any purchase\nsurvey_features['ENG_HAS_PURCHASE'] = (survey_features['ENG_NUM_ORDERS'] > 0).astype(int)\n\n# Fill remaining missing values\nsurvey_features['ENG_AGE_NUMERIC'] = survey_features['ENG_AGE_NUMERIC'].fillna(survey_features['ENG_AGE_NUMERIC'].median())\nsurvey_features['ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC'] = survey_features['ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC'].fillna(survey_features['ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC'].median())\n\n# Select and standardize engineered features\nengineered_cols = [col for col in survey_features.columns pct_change = (observed - predicted) / abs(predicted)\n\n    if pct_change > 0:\n        # Surge: scale to 0-100\n        anomaly_index = min(100, (pct_change / (pct_change + 0.1)) * 100)\n    else:\n        # Slump: scale to -100-0\n        anomaly_index = max(-100, (pct_change / (abs(pct_change) + 0.1)) * 100)\n\n    return anomaly_index\n\n# Calculate anomalies\nanomalies = {}\ncategory_metrics = ]\n\n    if len(baseline) == 0 or len(treatment) == 0:\n        continue\n\n    # Calculate baseline statistics\n    baseline_mean = baseline['Daily_Sales'].mean()\n    baseline_std = baseline['Daily_Sales'].std()\n\n    # Actual March 2020 sales\n    actual_march_sales = treatment['Daily_Sales'].sum()\n\n    # Counterfactual: expected sales based on baseline average\n    predicted_march_sales = baseline_mean * len(treatment)\n\n    # Calculate anomaly index\n    anomaly_idx = calculate_anomaly_index(actual_march_sales, predicted_march_sales)\n    anomalies[category] = E-Commerce Anomaly Analysis: March 2020 Report\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nThis analysis identifies product categories that showed anomalous daily sales patterns in March 2020 (treatment period) compared to January-February 2020 (baseline period), and determines what demographic factors explain these anomalies using Difference-in-Differences causal analysis.\n\n### Key Findings\n\n**Anomaly Detection**:\n- Identified **244 product categories** with available data\n- **Top 10 Surge Categories** (unusual increases):\n  - PROTECTIVE_GLOVE (96.34 anomaly index)\n  - CHAIR (93.91)\n  - MEDICATION (93.09)\n  - CRACKER (93.04)\n  - SKIN_CLEANING_WIPE (92.68)\n  - TOILET_PAPER (92.49)\n  - PET_FEEDER (92.34)\n  - NOODLE (91.24)\n  - CLEANING_AGENT (90.53)\n  - PUZZLES (90.20)\n\n- **Top 10 Slump Categories** (unusual decreases):\n  - DIGITAL_DEVICE_4 (-86.46 anomaly index)\n  - WATCH (-85.21)\n  - PERSONAL_FRAGRANCE (-84.63)\n  - PLANNER (-84.12)\n  - MEAL_HOLDER (-83.65)\n  - COSTUME_OUTFIT (-83.01)\n  - WIRELESS_ACCESSORY (-82.93)\n  - COAT (-82.76)\n  - HANDBAG (-80.62)\n  - POWER_BANK (-80.45)\n\n### Business Interpretation\n\n**Surge Categories Narrative**: The spike in protective gloves, cleaning supplies, medication, and pantry items (crackers, noodles) during March 2020 aligns with the COVID-19 pandemic's initial phase when consumers rushed to purchase protective equipment, cleaning products, and shelf-stable food items.\n\n**Slump Categories Narrative**: The decline in fashion items (coats, handbags), accessories (watches, wireless accessories), luxury goods (fragrance), and items dependent on office/travel use (planners, digital devices) reflects reduced discretionary spending and work-from-home adoption.\n\n---\n\n## Data Pipeline Overview\n\n### Step 1: Data Cleaning\n\n#### Survey Data (`survey_cleaned.csv`)\n- **Input**: 5,027 survey responses with 17 demographic/behavioral columns\n- **Output**: 5,027 cleaned responses\n- **Cleaning Actions**:\n  - Removed 0 exact duplicates\n  - Retained 100% of records with valid identifiers\n  - No missing values in critical ID columns\n\n#### Transaction Data (`amazon-purchases-2019-2020-filtered.csv`)\n- **Input**: 485,370 purchase transactions\n- **Output**: 481,288 valid transactions\n- **Cleaning Actions**:\n  - Removed 3,909 duplicate transactions category for 2 years of historical data\n- For March 2020, compared actual spending vs. counterfactual prediction based on baseline period average\n- Computed anomaly index on [-100, 100] scale:\n  - **100** = extreme unusual surge\n  - **0** = normal spending\n  - **-100** = extreme unusual slump\n\n**Formula**:\n```\nAnomaly_Index = {\n  100 × (% change) / (% change + 0.1)  if positive change,\n  -100 × (|% change|) / (|% change| + 0.1)  if negative change\n}\n```\n\n### Step 3: Feature Engineering\n\n**Engineered Demographics** (`survey_feature_engineered.csv`):\n1. **ENG_AGE_NUMERIC**: Mapped age brackets to midpoints (18-85 years)\n2. **ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC**: Mapped income brackets to midpoints ($12.5K-$175K)\n3. **ENG_FEMALE**: Binary indicator (0/1)\n4. **ENG_COLLEGE_EDU**: Binary indicator for Bachelor's degree or higher (0/1)\n5. **ENG_FREQUENT_AMAZON_USER**: Binary indicator for frequent use (0/1)\n6. **ENG_TOTAL_SPEND**: Total historical spending 2019-2020\n7. **ENG_AVG_ORDER_VALUE**: Average transaction amount\n8. **ENG_NUM_ORDERS**: Count of transactions\n9. **ENG_NUM_CATEGORIES**: Count of distinct categories purchased\n10. **ENG_HAS_PURCHASE**: Binary (`user_category_period_aggregated_intensive.csv`):\n- Unit of observation: Customer-Category-Period\n- Outcome: Total spending in category during period (among purchasers)\n- 2,676 records analyzed purchase in category during period\n- 2,676 records analyzed across 20 categories × 2 periods\n\n---\n\n## Causal Analysis Results\n\n### Methodology: Difference-in-Differences (DiD)\n\n**Design**:\n- **Treatment Group**: Customers with high values on demographic feature\n- **Control Group**: Customers with low values on demographic feature\n- **Pre-Period**: January-February 2020 (Baseline)\n- **Post-Period**: March 2020 (Treatment)\n\n**DiD Estimator**:\n```\nDiD = (Y_treated,post - Y_treated,pre) - (Y_control,post - Y_control,pre)\n```\n\nThis estimates the causal impact of demographic characteristics on spending changes during the anomaly period.\n\n### Top Demographic Drivers\n\nThe analysis identified Top 3 demographic factors driving both:\n1. **Intensive Margin**: Changes in spending amount among purchasers\n2. **Extensive Margin**: Changes in probability of purchase\n\n#### Surge Category Example: CHAIR\n- **Anomaly Index**: 93.91 (major surge)\n- **Baseline Avg Spend**: $114.51\n- **Treatment Avg Spend**: $175.11\n- **Spending Increase**: 52.9%\n\n**Intensive Margin Drivers**:\n1. **ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC**: DiD = +$188.67 (p=0.169)\n2. **ENG_NUM_CATEGORIES**: DiD = -$169.50 (p=0.203)\n3. **ENG_FEMALE**: DiD = +$152.53 (p=0.256)\n\n**Interpretation**: Higher income customers increased chair spending most dramatically; frequent category shoppers actually decreased Mobility**: Watches, handbags, coats declined as people reduced out-of-home activities\n2. **Discretionary Spending Cuts**: Fashion and accessories (handbags, fragrance) postponed\n3. **Changed Work E-Commerce Anomaly Analysis: March 2020 Report\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nThis analysis identifies product categories that showed anomalous daily sales patterns in March 2020 (treatment period) compared to January-February 2020 (baseline period), and determines what demographic factors explain these anomalies using Difference-in-Differences causal analysis.\n\n### Key Findings\n\n**Anomaly Detection**:\n- Identified **244 product categories** with available data\n- **Top 10 Surge Categories** (unusual increases):\n  - PROTECTIVE_GLOVE (96.34 anomaly index)\n  - CHAIR (93.91)\n  - MEDICATION (93.09)\n  - CRACKER (93.04)\n  - SKIN_CLEANING_WIPE (92.68)\n  - TOILET_PAPER (92.49)\n  - PET_FEEDER (92.34)\n  - NOODLE (91.24)\n  - CLEANING_AGENT (90.53)\n  - PUZZLES (90.20)\n\n- **Top 10 Slump Categories** (unusual decreases):\n  - DIGITAL_DEVICE_4 (-86.46 anomaly index)\n  - WATCH (-85.21)\n  - PERSONAL_FRAGRANCE (-84.63)\n  - PLANNER (-84.12)\n  - MEAL_HOLDER (-83.65)\n  - COSTUME_OUTFIT (-83.01)\n  - WIRELESS_ACCESSORY (-82.93)\n  - COAT (-82.76)\n  - HANDBAG (-80.62)\n  - POWER_BANK (-80.45)\n\n### Business Interpretation\n\n**Surge Categories Narrative**: The spike in protective gloves, cleaning supplies, medication, and pantry items (crackers, noodles) during March 2020 aligns with the COVID-19 pandemic's initial phase when consumers rushed to purchase protective equipment, cleaning products, and shelf-stable food items.\n\n**Slump Categories Narrative**: The decline in fashion items (coats, handbags), accessories (watches, wireless accessories), luxury goods (fragrance), and items dependent on office/travel use (planners, digital devices) reflects reduced discretionary spending and work-from-home adoption.\n\n---\n\n## Data Pipeline Overview\n\n### Step 1: Data Cleaning\n\n#### Survey Data (`survey_cleaned.csv`)\n- **Input**: 5,027 survey responses with 17 demographic/behavioral columns\n- **Output**: 5,027 cleaned responses\n- **Cleaning Actions**:\n  - Removed 0 exact duplicates\n  - Retained 100% of records with valid identifiers\n  - No missing values in critical ID columns\n\n#### Transaction Data (`amazon-purchases-2019-2020-filtered.csv`)\n- **Input**: 485,370 purchase transactions\n- **Output**: 481,288 valid transactions\n- **Cleaning Actions**:\n  - Removed 3,909 duplicate transactions (0.8%)\n  - Removed 0 records with missing dates/ResponseID\n  - Validated purchase amounts > $0 (100% valid)\n  - Validated quantities > 0 (100% valid)\n  - Imputed 43,540 missing categories as \"UNCATEGORIZED\"\n  - Retained 244 distinct product categories\n  - Filtered to 2019-2020 date range\n\n### Step 2: Anomaly Detection\n\n**Methodology**:\n- Calculated daily sales aggregates by category for 2 years of historical data\n- For March 2020, compared actual spending vs. counterfactual prediction based on baseline period average\n- Computed anomaly index on [-100, 100] scale:\n  - **100** = extreme unusual surge\n  - **0** = normal spending\n  - **-100** = extreme unusual slump\n\n**Formula**:\n```\nAnomaly_Index = {\n  100 × (% change) / (% change + 0.1)  if positive change,\n  -100 × (|% change|) / (|% change| + 0.1)  if negative change\n}\n```\n\n### Step 3: Feature Engineering\n\n**Engineered Demographics** (`survey_feature_engineered.csv`):\n1. **ENG_AGE_NUMERIC**: Mapped age brackets to midpoints (18-85 years)\n2. **ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC**: Mapped income brackets to midpoints ($12.5K-$175K)\n3. **ENG_FEMALE**: Binary indicator (0/1)\n4. **ENG_COLLEGE_EDU**: Binary indicator for Bachelor's degree or higher (0/1)\n5. **ENG_FREQUENT_AMAZON_USER**: Binary indicator for frequent use (0/1)\n6. **ENG_TOTAL_SPEND**: Total historical spending 2019-2020\n7. **ENG_AVG_ORDER_VALUE**: Average transaction amount\n8. **ENG_NUM_ORDERS**: Count of transactions\n9. **ENG_NUM_CATEGORIES**: Count of distinct categories purchased\n10. **ENG_HAS_PURCHASE**: Binary indicator for any purchase (0/1)\n\nAll numerical features scaled to mean=0, std=1 for interpretability.\n\n### Step 4: Causal Analysis Data Preparation\n\n**Intensive Margin** (`user_category_period_aggregated_intensive.csv`):\n- Unit of observation: Customer-Category-Period\n- Outcome: Total spending in category during period (among purchasers)\n- 2,676 records analyzed across 20 categories × 2 periods\n\n**Extensive Margin** (`user_category_period_aggregated_extensive.csv`):\n- Unit of observation: Customer-Category-Period\n- Outcome: Binary indicator of any purchase in category during period\n- 2,676 records analyzed across 20 categories × 2 periods\n\n---\n\n## Causal Analysis Results\n\n### Methodology: Difference-in-Differences (DiD)\n\n**Design**:\n- **Treatment Group**: Customers with high values on demographic feature\n- **Control Group**: Customers with low values on demographic feature\n- **Pre-Period**: January-February 2020 (Baseline)\n- **Post-Period**: March 2020 (Treatment)\n\n**DiD Estimator**:\n```\nDiD = (Y_treated,post - Y_treated,pre) - (Y_control,post - Y_control,pre)\n```\n\nThis estimates the causal impact of demographic characteristics on spending changes during the anomaly period.\n\n### Top Demographic Drivers\n\nThe analysis identified Top 3 demographic factors driving both:\n1. **Intensive Margin**: Changes in spending amount among purchasers\n2. **Extensive Margin**: Changes in probability of purchase\n\n#### Surge Category Example: CHAIR\n- **Anomaly Index**: 93.91 (major surge)\n- **Baseline Avg Spend**: $114.51\n- **Treatment Avg Spend**: $175.11\n- **Spending Increase**: 52.9%\n\n**Intensive Margin Drivers**:\n1. **ENG_INCOME_NUMERIC**: DiD = +$188.67 (p=0.169)\n2. **ENG_NUM_CATEGORIES**: DiD = -$169.50 (p=0.203)\n3. **ENG_FEMALE**: DiD = +$152.53 (p=0.256)\n\n**Interpretation**: Higher income customers increased chair spending most dramatically; frequent category shoppers actually decreased spending; female customers showed higher spending increases.\n\n---\n\n## Output Files Description\n\n| File | Records | Description |\n|------|---------|-------------|\n| `survey_cleaned.csv` | 5,027 | Cleaned survey responses with 17 columns |\n| `amazon-purchases-2019-2020-filtered.csv` | 481,288 | Cleaned transactions with Date, Category, Amount, Quantity |\n| `category_anomaly_index.csv` | 244 Customer-Category-Period spending for DiD analysis |\n| `user_category_period_aggregated_extensive.csv` | 2,676 | Customer-Category-Period purchase indicators for DiD |\n| `causal_analysis_report.json` | 1 | Structured causal analysis results for all 20 categories |\n\n---\n\n## Technical Specifications\n\n### Time Periods\n- **Baseline**: 01-01-2020 to 02-29-2020 (60 days)\n- **Treatment**: 03-01-2020 to 03-31-2020 (31 days)\n\n### Sample Sizes\n- **Total Customers**: 5,027 survey respondents\n- **Transactions in Analysis Period**: 56,675 (11.8% of all 2019-2020 transactions)\n- **Top 20 Categories Analyzed**:\n  - Average customers per category: ~100\n  - Average purchasers per period: ~80\n\n### Features Analyzed\n- **Total Engineered Features**: 20\n- **Features in DiD Analysis**: 10 core demographic indicators\n- **Surge Categories**: 10\n- **Slump Categories**: 10\n- **Total DiD Estimates**: 600 (10 features × 20 categories × 3 estimates per margin)\n\n---\n\n## Key Insights\n\n### Why These Categories Surged (Protective Equipment & Essentials)\n1. **Health Concerns**: Protective gloves, medication, and cleaning products surged due to COVID-19 panic buying\n2. **Home Confinement**: Chairs and furniture for home office setup increased as work-from-home adoption accelerated\n3. **Food Security**: Shelf-stable pantry items (crackers, noodles) purchased as precaution\n4. **Entertainment**: Puzzles purchased for home entertainment during lockdowns\n\n### Why These Categories Slumped (Discretionary & Mobility-Dependent)\n1. **Reduced Mobility**: Watches, handbags, coats declined as people reduced out-of-home activities\n2. **Discretionary Spending Cuts**: Fashion and accessories (handbags, fragrance) postponed\n3. **Changed Work Patterns**: Digital devices (4) and planners used less as office work transitioned\n4. **Reduced Dining Out**: Meal holders less relevant during lockdown\n\n### Demographic Patterns\n- **Higher Income**: Stronger spending increases in high-value items (chairs, medication)\n- **Frequent Users**: Multi-category shoppers showed different adjustment patterns\n- **Gender Differences**: Female customers showed different surge responses in some categories\n- **Education & Age**: Impact varied by category, suggesting heterogeneous treatment effects\n\n---\n\n## Limitations & Caveats\n\n1. **Small Sample Sizes**: Some categories had <20 purchasers, limiting statistical power\n2. **Short Post-Period**: March 2020 is only 31 days; longer observation would strengthen conclusions\n3. **Missing Data**: 9% of transactions missing category labels (coded as \"UNCATEGORIZED\")\n4. **Parallel Trends**: Analysis assumes baseline trends would continue without pandemic (untestable)\n5. **Unobserved Confounding**: Other factors (promotions, supply constraints) not controlled\n6. **p-values**: High p-values (many >0.20) indicate wide confidence intervals; treat effect sizes as tentative\n\n---\n\n## Recommendations for Further Analysis\n\n1. **Causal Inference**: Consider instrumental variables or synthetic control methods for robustness\n2. **Temporal Dynamics**: Analyze how anomalies evolved through March and early April\n3. **Regional Analysis**: Examine whether COVID intensity varied causal effects by state\n4. **Price Effects**: Investigate if surges/slumps driven by price changes vs. quantity changes\n5. **Supply Constraints**: Check if anomalies reflect demand or supply-side disruptions\n6. **Competitor Analysis**: Compare Amazon anomalies with overall retail trends\n\n---\n\n## Analysis Execution Details\n\n- **Total Processing Time**: ~60 seconds\n- **Memory Usage**: ~500MB for combined dataset\n- **Language**: Python 3 with pandas, statsmodels, scipy\n-
